She has received fellowships from the Vermont Studio Center and the Massachusetts Cultural Council as well as the Ray Stark Film Prize. Her work has been featured in the New York Times, Forbes, Vogue, ArtNet, Curbed, and Time Out, among others. name=\"offerings_update[body]\" id=\"offerings_update_form_2029_offerings_update_body\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eKarline Moeller the founder of Art Frankly a Global \u003cem\u003eart\u003c/em\u003e community to post \u003cem\u003eart\u003c/em\u003e jobs, find spaces and discover opportunities. \u0026nbsp; Sat down with us for an interview on our experiences in the art world and creating Alpha'a.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003e\"This weekend we sit down with not one, but two amazing women, Manuela Seve and Renata Thomé, the ladies who founded and run\u0026nbsp;\u003ca href=\"https://www.alphaainc.com/home\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eAlpha’a\u003c/a\u003e. Alpha’a is a crowdsourced purpose driven art platform, whose mission is to revolutionize the arts, whilst honoring the profoundly human aspects of collecting. Manuela is founder and CEO of Alpha’a. With a background in finance, Manuela worked as an equity analyst at Gávea Investments, one of Brazil’s most reputable investment management firms. Manuela’s trajectory in the art world began at an early age; she comes from a long line of art collectors with a foothold in the Latin American art market. Born in Rio de Janeiro, Manuela received her Economics degree from IBMEC there. Renata is partner and CCO of Alpha’a. Renata brings an extensive network of collectors, artists and dealers to her work. Before joining Alpha’a she worked for major New York City galleries and auction houses including David Zwirner and in the Latin American art department at Christie’s. Previously, Renata worked as an art consultant for Paddle8. Renata was born in Sao Paulo and both she and Manuela currently live in New York City. Please enjoy this unique Frank Talk below!\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eWhat was your first job in the Arts?\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eM:\u0026nbsp;\u003c/strong\u003eWhen I graduated high school at 17, my dad which at the time lived in NY (I was in Brazil) had a heart attack, I moved to the city to help him run his gallery, that was how it all started.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eR:\u0026nbsp;\u003c/strong\u003eWhile I was finishing my master’s degree at Christie’s I ended up taking an internship at the auction house, working in the Latin American art department. That was an amazing ‘real life’ school for me and I’m still in contact with some of my supervisors from that time.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eWhat was the most useful or important thing you learned at that job?\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eM:\u0026nbsp;\u003c/strong\u003eThe market lacked transparency in terms of pricing, and provenance was a major issue. \u0026nbsp;Opportunities for up and coming artists were also extremely limited, I could tell that meritocracy was many times less important than relationships.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eR:\u0026nbsp;\u003c/strong\u003eThat there was a lot that could be done in order to improve research and cataloguing when it comes to art. Catalogue Raisonnes are hard to find and expensive and when it comes to Latin American artists it becomes even harder. I’m thankful for seeing all this from up-close, something which motivated me to start planning tools which we now use at Alpha’a.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eTell us a little more about yourself. When did you realize you wanted to pursue a career in this industry?\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eM:\u0026nbsp;\u003c/strong\u003eI moved back to Brazil after 2 years in NY, studied economics and went into finance, At the time I was reading Marc Rich’s book, “King of Oil,” and looking at the oil market in the 60s and comparing it to the way the art market still operates (limited number of players calling shots, lack of price transparency, etc.) there were so many similarities that an opportunity to disrupt it was very clear.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eR:\u0026nbsp;\u003c/strong\u003eWhen I moved to NY and started spending a lot of time with some friends who already worked in the sector. This city lives and breathes art and it was easy for me to start getting involved.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eWhat do you do now?\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eM:\u0026nbsp;\u003c/strong\u003eI’m CEO and co-founder of Alpha’a, we’re a b2b marketplace connecting companies to the visual arts.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eR:\u0026nbsp;\u003c/strong\u003eI’m a co-founder and Chief Creative Officer for Alpha’a.\"\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eRead the full interview \u003ca show\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eNew York City - March 23, 2020 --\u0026nbsp;\u003ca href=\"https://www.alphaainc.com/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eAlpha’a\u003c/a\u003e, the B2B, crowd-sourced and purpose-driven PropTech fine art sales platform, has advanced to the second round of\u0026nbsp;\u003ca href=\"https://www.meetthedrapers.com/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eMeet The Drapers\u003c/a\u003e after winning in its first appearance on the popular tech startup investment television show.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eA groundbreaking reality show in Silicon Valley, Meet The Drapers is \u0026nbsp;where viewers get to decide the next big idea in tech. The legendary Draper family meets the world's hottest new entrepreneurs on the hunt for the next billion-dollar idea. The show scours the world for the best entrepreneurs and selects the top 27. The selected founders get up close and personal with three generations of the Drapers and celebrity guest judges to fight for a spot in the grand finale.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e“We are thrilled to have impressed the judges with the Alpha’a platform,” said Manuela Seve, Co-Founder \u0026amp; CEO of the startup, who appeared on the show with her Co-Founder \u0026amp; CCO, Renata Thome. “It brings us one step closer to our dream of making fine art available to everyone. They really got our origin story. How I saw the connection between the oil market in the ‘60s and the fine art market now, and that they both needed to bring more buyers together with creators.”\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eOne of Meet The Drapers’ great values is that it gives viewers the opportunity to invest in the competing companies and to be part of the next ground-breaking firms from Silicon Valley and beyond. Individuals interested in investing in Alpha’a can go to\u0026nbsp;\u003ca href=\"https://republic.co/alpha-a\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eRepublic’s\u003c/a\u003e funding platform to do so. The deadline for investing on this platform is\u0026nbsp;April 30, 2020.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eAlpha’a, a New York City based and Brazil originated startup, was a member of MetaProp’s 2019-20 accelerator cohort. The firm has demonstrated proven ability to quickly assemble and install on-demand, high-quality curated, limited-edition prints for the hospitality, office and other real estate sectors.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/textarea\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class='s-marginTop1_5 id=\"offerings_update_form_1801_offerings_update_body\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eDear Investors and Followers,\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eNeedless to say that the current economic environment has changed, the Coronavirus outbreak is what we economists call a black swan, an unexpected event that will bring widespread adverse effects to economic activity.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eWe at Alpha'a are preparing for the worst and expecting the best, we have stress tested our financial models and we're taking into account 5 months of zero revenue (an extreme scenario we know), we are also implementing cost cutting measures to preserve capital, for example: halting business travel and waiting to hire more staff. \u0026nbsp;Under this extreme scenario we still have enough cash in the bank till early 2021, not considering the funds raised here, which will expand our run rate at least until 2022.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eBut it is not all gloom, troubled times bring huge opportunities, not only for hiring great talent at a fraction of the cost, but for changing old business models, and if there was ever an old business model the art market for real estate takes the prize. \u0026nbsp;Galleries are already shutting down for in person visits meaning that people now have to explore virtual collections, a feature that we are very familiar with and have already built out and tested.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eHospitality companies have endless projects that need to be finished and more cost effective solutions like Alpha'a will be necessary. \u0026nbsp;Real estate companies are searching for alternative sources of revenue for which our QR code based rev share model is ideal, and finally people working from home will want to add some color into their spaces. \u0026nbsp;We are already thinking of all of these solutions and campaigns and need your help to continue growing, we believe we are uniquely positioned to face this changing world and that competition in the sector which relies on traditional brick and mortar initiatives will diminish greatly.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThank you for supporting us along this journey and lets continue to change the way people interact with walls.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eAll the best,\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eManuela\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/textarea\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv SEVE\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eManuela Seve is a founder, entrepreneur, triathlete, musician and art lover who is revolutionizing the arts, whilst honoring the profoundly human aspects of collecting. What started as a t shirt competition at an art fair in Brazil has democratized and modernized the art acquisition process through the first crowd sourced art \u0026amp; design platform with over 9,000 artists from around the world. This purpose driven art platform is working across 3 continents, has sold over 1,100 pieces of art in the past 2 years, is the world's biggest inventory free art gallery, and has a clientele from the 6 billion dollar hospitality industry such as Zola, Gilt, and Hilton.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eListen to the full interview \u003ca push the world to Evolve.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eMake it a prettier place for sure. id=\"offerings_update_form_1428_edit_offerings_update_1428\" action=\"/offerings/updates/alpha-a-manuela-seve-propmodo-features-alpha-a-on-how-blockchain-is-helping\" accept-charset=\"UTF-8\" id=\"offerings_update_form_1428_offerings_update_body\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.propmodo.com/how-blockchain-is-helping-bring-fine-art-to-buildings/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eHow Blockchain is Helping Bring Fine Art to Buildings\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eIn 2018, a groundbreaking report from The Alan Turing Institute detailed how using blockchain would fundamentally improve the authentication and value of fine artworks. The report,\u0026nbsp;\u003ca href=\"https://www.dacs.org.uk/latest-news/report-art-market-powered-by-blockchain?category=For+Artists\u0026amp;title=N\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eThe Art Market 2.0: Blockchain and Financialisation in Visual Arts\u003c/a\u003e, introduced the benefits of bringing digital ledger technologies to the UK art market, with the proposed potential for a soaring increase in art market liquidity and value. Since then, the use of blockchain to prove the provenance of artworks and enhance their worth has grown worldwide. It has also made acquiring fine art for buildings even more attractive for owners and developers looking to add this rarefied amenity to their properties.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eAs the New York City experiential website 6sqft\u0026nbsp;\u003ca href=\"https://www.6sqft.com/art-and-real-estate-the-new-relationship-thats-selling-big/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003enoted\u003c/a\u003e, luxury apartment developers such as Aby Rosen, traditional brokerages like Douglas Elliman and more tech-forward ones like Compass see the value of art in attracting and selling to tenants. As architect Morris Adjimi says in the 6sqft story: “Art is more stimulating to me than the latest trends in architecture. Art is visceral and topical, it is much more immediate and it allows you to get into the zeitgeist of the time. In art, we see what is happening now, in architecture it takes a few years to show up.”\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThe 6sgft story further asserts that: More and more architects, developers, designers, and brokers believe in the powerful relationship between art and real estate. So much so, it is now understood that art sells real estate and real estate sells art.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eIn fact, discerning real estate developers and owners are becoming increasingly aware that adding fine artworks to their property can be a major differentiator and value-add in attracting tenants. The commercial real estate world is also part of the trend. It’s virtually impossible to walk into the lobby of any modern urban office building without seeing a painting, mural or sculpture adorning the lobby. The centrality of art to office buildings goes back at least to the early 1930s, when the Rockefellers commissioned (and later fired for his work being too politically radical to their taste) famed artist Diego Rivera to paint some of the murals in Rockefeller Center.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eHowever, until recently, unless you were “as rich as Rockefeller,” as the old song goes, a variety of issues made it difficult and expensive for most owners to bring quality fine art into buildings. Part of the problem involved authenticating the artworks, which would help assure their value and cost-effectiveness. Here is where technology has inserted itself to solve the problem, as blockchain technologists have created platforms to bring transparency and increased liquidity to the marketplace for fine art in real estate.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eIn the last two years, the PropTech startup \u003ca href=\"https://www.alphaainc.com/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eAlpha’a\u003c/a\u003e, an online, community-oriented platform offering tailor-made art collections for businesses across all industries, including the real estate sectors of hospitality, office, and co-working/living spaces, has addressed this complex challenge for property owners and developers....\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eRead the whole article \u003ca href=\"https://www.propmodo.com/how-blockchain-is-helping-bring-fine-art-to-buildings/\"
